LinearRecurrence.IsSolutiondefMathlib.Algebra.LinearRecurrence, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $E$ be a linear recurrence of $R$. Let $u$ be a function from natural numbers to elements of $R$. Then $\operatorname{IsSolution}$ holds for $E$ and $u$, if for all natural numbers $n$, $u$ applied to the sum of $n$ and the order of $E$ is equal to $\FinsetSum {\FinsetunivMACRoAC}{i}{(\operatorname{coeffs} (E , i)\times u (n + \operatorname{val} (i)))}$.
Formula
$E \in \mathrm{LinearRecurrence} (R), u \in \Natural \rightarrow R \vdash \operatorname{IsSolution} (E , u):\Leftrightarrow \forall n \in \Natural . \EqtMACRoJ {u (n + \operatorname{order} (E))}{\FinsetSum {\FinsetunivMACRoAJ}{i}{(\operatorname{coeffs} (E , i)\times u (n + \operatorname{val} (i)))}}$

SnakeLemma.δdefMathlib.Algebra.Module.SnakeLemma, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $x_{19}$ be a linear map of $x_{1}$ and $x_{4}$. Let $x_{20}$ be a linear map of $x_{2}$ and $x_{5}$. Let $x_{21}$ be a linear map of $x_{3}$ and $x_{6}$. Let $x_{22}$ be a linear map of $x_{1}$ and $x_{2}$. Let $x_{23}$ be a linear map of $x_{2}$ and $x_{3}$ such that $\operatorname{Exact}$ holds for $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $x_{22}$ and $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $x_{23}$. Let $x_{24}$ be a linear map of $x_{4}$ and $x_{5}$. Let $x_{25}$ be a linear map of $x_{5}$ and $x_{6}$ such that $\operatorname{Exact}$ holds for $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $x_{24}$ and $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $x_{25}$.
